\pi_{n}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell})
holds. The description of the Frobenius now follows from
4.4.15
, the observation that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(X_{g})_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{dg};X_{dg})_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(X_{dg})_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{0})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{0})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

is visibly commutative and, moreover, the right vertical map is
C_{d}
-equivariant, and the fact that
r
is injective on rational homotopy groups by the discussion above.
∎

5.3
Homotopy of embedding spaces of
(Y_{g};X_{g})

We proceed analogously to
Section

4.5
to compute (up to some ambiguities) the rational homotopy groups of
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}
in a range, using the fibre sequence (
5.0.1
). Once again, there is one connecting map in the long exact sequence of (
5.0.1
) that we will need to consider. The following lemma is analogous to
Lemma

4.5.1
, and its proof is completely identical.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

The following connecting map is zero:

\delta_{n}^{Y}:\pi_{n}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\mathrm{B}\widetilde{\Emb}{\vphantom{\Emb}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}\right)\longrightarrow\pi_{n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\big(\widetilde{\Emb}/\mathrm{Emb}\big)_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})\right).

From the result above,
5.1.5
, and the long exact sequence of (
5.0.1
), we obtain the following.

Proposition 5.3.2
.

The rational homotopy groups of
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}
in degrees
2\leq*\leq 2n-2
are given as
\mathbf{Q}[\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}\rtimes\langle\rho\rangle]
-modules by

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}\right)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\bigoplus_{r>0}(H_{g}\oplus H_{V_{g}})&*=n-1,\\[4.0pt]
\pi_{n}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BAut_{\partial^{v}}^{\cong}(Y_{g};X_{g}))&*=n,\\[3.0pt]
\coker(\delta_{2n-1}^{Y})&*=2n-2\\[3.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

5.4
Homology of embedding spaces of
(Y_{g};X_{g})

In this section, we compute the homotopy groups of
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}
in roughly in the range
*\leq 2n-2
. As in
Section

4.6
, we start by computing the homology of the universal cover of the latter space before taking the plus-construction and colimit over
g
, which we denote by
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim}.

Notation 5.4.1
.

Let
L^{Y},M^{Y},N^{Y}
denote the
\mathbf{Q}[\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}\rtimes\langle\rho\rangle]
-modules

L^{Y}\coloneq\pi_{n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}\right),\hskip 15.0ptM^{Y}\coloneq\pi_{n}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}\right),\hskip 15.0ptN^{Y}\coloneq\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}\right).

The following computation follows verbatim as
4.6.2
.

Proposition 5.4.2
.

The rational homology groups of
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim}
in degrees
*\leq 2n-2
are given as
\mathbf{Q}[\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}\rtimes\langle\rho\rangle]
-modules by

\widetilde{\H}_{*}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim};\mathbf{Q}\right)\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}L^{Y}&*=n-1,\\[4.0pt]
M^{Y}&*=n,\\[4.0pt]
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\oplus\coker(d:L^{Y}\otimes M^{Y}\to N^{Y})&*=2n-2,\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{array}\right.

where
d
is a certain map.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
denote the
\mathbf{Q}[\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}\rtimes\langle\rho\rangle]
-module given by

\mathcal{L}_{Y}\coloneq\colim_{g}\smash{\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\oplus\coker(d:L^{Y}\otimes M^{Y}\to N^{Y})\right]_{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}

where
d
is the map from
5.4.2
. As before, conjugation by
\rho=(-1)^{\oplus g}
induces a
\langle\rho\rangle
-action on
\BGL_{g}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
, and hence on
\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
, that makes the map
\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty}\to\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
be
\langle\rho\rangle
-equivariant.

Theorem 5.4.3
.

In the range
*\leq 2n-2
, we have an isomorphism of
\mathbf{Q}[\langle\rho\rangle]
-modules

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}\right)\cong\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+}\right)\oplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathcal{L}_{Y}&*=2n-2,\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

We prove this statement by applying
Lemma

4.6.3
to the map
p:\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}\to\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+}
of simple spaces, by proving that this map induces an isomorphism on
\H_{*}(-;\mathbf{Q})
for
*\leq 2n-3
and a surjection in degrees
*=2n-2,2n-1.
As the map
(\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty})^{+}\to\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+}
is a rational equivalence by
3.3.3
, it suffices to prove the same claim for the map
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}\to(\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty})^{+}.
Consider the Serre spectral sequence of the universal cover fibre sequence

\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{\sim}\to\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}\to\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty}.

Once again as in the proof of
4.6.4
, the
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
-modules
L^{Y}
and
M^{Y}
are gr-odd in the sense of
3.4.1
. Thus, the rational homology of
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
with coefficients in
L^{Y}
and
M^{Y}
vanishes in all degrees. In the same way as in
4.6.4
, we conclude that the spectral sequence of the fibre sequence above has the following behaviour: for
t\leq 2n-2
, the group
E^{2}_{s,t}
vanishes if
t\neq 0,2n-2
. For
t=0
, we have
E^{2}_{s,0}\cong\H_{s}(\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty};\mathbf{Q})
. The entry
E^{2}_{0,2n-2}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{Y}.
From this behaviour, we automatically conclude that the map
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}\to\text{{B}}\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{\infty}
is an isomorphism on rational homology in degrees less than
2n-2
and surjective in degree
2n-2,
hence showing the first part of the original claim.

It remains to show that
\H_{2n-1}(p;\mathbf{Q})
is a surjection. From the discussion above, we know that
p
is rationally
(2n-2)
-connected, as it is a map of simple spaces that is homologically
(2n-2)
-connected. Recall from the proof of
4.6.4
that the homology of a connected
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-space is isomorphic as an algebra to the universal enveloping algebra of its rational homotopy Lie algebra. Thus, to show that
\H_{2n-1}(p;\mathbf{Q})
is surjective (and given that it is surjective on the lower degrees), it suffices to prove that
\pi_{2n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(p)
is surjective. Consider the following commutative square induced by the inclusion
\iota:(V_{g};W_{g,1})\hookrightarrow(Y_{g};X_{g})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(V_{\infty};W_{\infty,1})^{+}_{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z})^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+},}

(5.4.1)

where the top left corner is the path component of the stable framing given by the restriction of
\ell
along
\iota
, the left vertical map is given by extending embeddings and stable framings, and the right vertical map is induced by extension of scalars. We deduce that the bottom map is surjective on rational homotopy groups in degree
2n-1
by the following two observations:

•

The map
\pi_{2n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z})^{+})\to\pi_{2n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+})
is an isomorphism
: this follows by identifying the source and target with the
K
-theory groups
\K_{2n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})
and
\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{2n-1}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi]),
and by applying the Bass–Heller–Swan isomorphism and the fact that
\K_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})=0.

•

The map
\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(V_{\infty};W_{\infty,1})^{+}_{\ell}\to\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z})^{+}
is rationally
(3n-5)
-connected
: this follows from
[
55
, Thm. 7.1]
by replacing the source by the variant with stable framings instead of framings (which does not affect the result by Lemma 2.8 in loc.cit, and using the fact that
\ell
is the standard framing of Section 2.5.1 of loc.cit), taking the colimit over
g
on the source and plus-constructing (making it an
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-space), hence obtaining rational connectivity in the desired range.

Thus, we observe that the bottom map of (
5.4.1
) is surjective on rational homotopy in degree
2n-1
. Hence, we conclude that
\H_{2n-1}(p;\mathbf{Q})
is surjective. We now apply
Lemma

4.6.3
for the map
p
and the group
A=\mathcal{L}_{Y}
. To check that the kernel of
\H_{2n-1}(p;\mathbf{Q})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
, we proceed verbatim as in
4.6.4
. Hence, we obtain the desired isomorphism as
\mathbf{Q}[\langle\rho\rangle]
-modules, which finishes the proof.
∎

5.5
The sources of infinite generation
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}

In this subsection, we analyse the
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
from
4.6.4
and
5.4.3
. The main result of this subsection (and the only one carried to the remaining sections) is the following.

Theorem 5.5.1
.

The induced map
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})\to\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})
satisfies the following properties:

(i)

The map is surjective.

(ii)

The kernel and target are infinitely generated as a
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces.

(iii)

The summand
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
of the source is in the
(-1)
-eigenspace of the reflection involution
\rho
.

To prove this result, we focus on the
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
. First, observe that, under the identifications of
4.6.4
and
5.4.3
, the map in
5.5.1
, restricts to the map
\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X}
which is given by taking colimits along
g
of the maps

\widetilde{\H}_{2n-2}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim};\mathbf{Q}\right)\to\widetilde{\H}_{2n-2}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim};\mathbf{Q}\right)

(5.5.1)

after taking coinvariants by
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
on the source and
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
on the target (recall
4.6.2
and
5.4.2
). We conclude that the map (
5.5.1
) is an extension of the following maps (recall
4.6.1
and
5.4.1
):

1

The map
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\to S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
induced by the projection
L^{Y}=\bigoplus_{r>0}(H_{g}\oplus H_{V_{g}})\to\bigoplus_{r>0}H_{g}
in each summand.

2

The induced map on the cokernels of the connecting maps
\delta
and
\delta^{Y}
of
4.5.2
and
5.3.2
, respectively, induced by the map
\smash{\big(\widetilde{\Emb}/\mathrm{Emb}\big)_{\partial_{0}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})\to\smash{\big(\widetilde{\Emb}/\mathrm{Emb}\big)_{\partial_{0}}}(X_{g})
on
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}.

We start by estimating the rank of the coinvariants of
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})
and
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
as modules over
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
and
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
, which is the key ingredient to establish
(ii)
of
5.5.1
. More precisely, we establish the following result. A directed system
A_{1}\to A_{2}\to\cdots
is
stably infinitely generated
if the its colimit
A_{\infty}
is infinitely generated.

Lemma 5.5.2
.

The systems of
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces
\smash{\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\right]_{\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}}
and
\smash{\ker\left(\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\right]_{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}\to\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\right]_{\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}\right)}
indexed by
g\geq 0
are stably infinitely generated.

Proof.

We start by proving that
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
-coinvariants of
\smash{S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)}
are infinitely generated, for every
g\geq 1
. First, we observe that the
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
-action on the latter vector space factors through the map
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}\to\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
: we see that it factors through
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}\to\mathrm{h}\Xi_{g}
where
\mathrm{h}\Xi_{g}=\pi_{0}(\Aut_{*}(X_{g}))
, as a consequence of
4.3.14
. Moreover, recall from
4.2.3
that the
\mathbf{Q}[\mathrm{h}\Xi_{g}]
-module
L=\bigoplus_{r>0}H_{g}
is a direct sum of the
\mathbf{Q}[\mathrm{h}\Xi_{g}]
-modules
H_{g}
. Now, these
\mathbf{Q}[\mathrm{h}\Xi_{g}]
-modules clearly factor through the map
\pi_{0}(\Aut_{*}(X_{g}))\to\pi_{0}(\Aut_{*}(W_{g,1}))\cong\GL_{2g}(\mathbf{Z}).
Thus, the action of
\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
on
L
factors through the map to
\GL_{2g}(\mathbf{Z})
and thus, it factors through the map to
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
. Thus, it suffices to prove that the
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
-coinvariants of this module are infinitely generated. To establish this claim, we produce a surjective map
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\to I
of
\mathbf{Q}[\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})]
-modules, where
I
is infinitely generated as a
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space and whose
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
-action is trivial. Denote the
r
-th
H_{g}
summand of
L
by
\smash{H_{g}^{(r)}}
. Let
\lambda:H_{g}\otimes H_{g}\to\mathbf{Q}
be the standard
(-1)^{n}
-hyperbolic form, then let
\smash{\lambda_{L}:L\otimes L\to\bigoplus_{r,s>0}\mathbf{Q}}
be given by the sum
\smash{\lambda^{(r,s)}=\lambda:H_{g}^{(r)}\otimes H_{g}^{(s)}\to\mathbf{Q}^{(r,s)}}
, using the isomorphism
\smash{L\otimes L\cong\bigoplus_{r,s>0}H_{g}^{(r)}\otimes H_{g}^{(s)}}
. Observe that
\lambda_{L}
is
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
-equivariant (where the target is seen with trivial action), since by definition the isomorphisms in
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
commute with the
(-1)^{n}
-hyperbolic form. Now, let
\sigma_{L}:L\otimes L\to L\otimes L
be the involution that interchanges the coordinates and acts by
(-1)^{n-1}
. Recall that
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
is, by definition, the cokernel of
\sigma_{L}.
We can now see that the following square commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\otimes L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{r,s>0}\mathbf{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\otimes L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{r,s>0}\mathbf{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{L}}

where
\sigma
is the involution given by the sum of the maps
-\id:\mathbf{Q}^{(r,s)}\to\mathbf{Q}^{(s,r)}
by the following argument: let
a,b\in H_{g}
and
a^{(r)},b^{(s)}
be the corresponding classes in
H_{g}^{(r)}
and
H_{g}^{(s)}
, then
\lambda_{L}(a^{(r)},b^{(s)})=(-1)^{n}\lambda_{L}(b^{(r)},a^{(s)})\in\mathbf{Q}^{(r,s)}.
Thus

\sigma(\lambda_{L}(a^{(r)},b^{(s)}))=-\lambda_{L}(a^{(s)},b^{(r)})=-(-1)^{n}\lambda(b^{(s)},a^{(r)})=(-1)^{n-1}\lambda_{L}(b^{(s)},a^{(r)})=\lambda_{L}(\sigma_{L}(a^{(r)},b^{(s)})).

Denote by
I
the quotient of
\bigoplus_{r,s>0}\mathbf{Q}
by
\sigma
, so the map
\lambda_{L}
descends to a
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
-equivariant map
\lambda_{L}:S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\to I
, where the action on
I
is trivial. Now, the map
\lambda_{L}
is surjective, since the
(-1)^{n}
-hyperbolic form
\lambda
is non-trivial. It remains to check that
I
is infinitely generated: the map
\sigma
identifies
\smash{\mathbf{Q}^{(r,s)}}
with
\smash{\mathbf{Q}^{(s,r)}}
, so
I
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{r>s>0}\mathbf{Q}
as a
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space, and hence infinitely generated. Thus, we conclude that the coinvariants of
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
are infinitely generated, as they admit a surjective map to an infinitely generated
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space
I
. Observe that the map
\lambda_{L}
is compatible with stabilisation induced by the inclusion
H_{g}\hookrightarrow H_{g+1}
. Thus, the map
\lambda_{L}
descends to a surjective map from the colimit to
I
. Hence, the colimit of this system is infinitely generated.

We proceed to prove, by a similar argument, that the second
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space in the statement is infinitely generated for every
g\geq 1
. Start by observing that it suffices to prove the same claim after we replace the target by the
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
-coinvariants of
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
, as the map
\smash{[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})]_{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}\to[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)]_{\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}}
factors through the map
\smash{[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})]_{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}\to[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)]_{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}
, induced by

1

. By the same argument as above, we see that the
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}
-representations
L^{Y}
and
L
factor through the map
\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}\to\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
, by using
5.1.5
. Moreover, this action is given by the sum of the actions on
H_{V_{g}}\oplus H_{g}
and
H_{g}
, respectively. As
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
-representations, we have a splitting
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\cong S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\oplus S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{\prime})\oplus(L\otimes L^{\prime})
, where
L^{\prime}\coloneq\bigoplus_{r>0}H_{V_{g}}.
Thus, the
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
-coinvariants of
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})
split as a sum of the coinvariants of these summands. As the map
S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})\to S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)
is induced by the projection
L^{Y}=L\oplus L^{\prime}\to L
, we see that the subspace
[L\otimes L^{\prime}]_{\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})}\subset[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})]_{\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})}
is contained in the kernel of
\smash{[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})]_{\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})}\to[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)]_{\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})}}.
Hence, we shall prove that this subspace is infinitely generated. Once again, it suffices to construct a surjective
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
-equivariant map
L\otimes L^{\prime}\to I
to an infinitely generated vector space, where
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
acts trivially on the target. We proceed similarly by defining the map
\smash{L\otimes L^{\prime}\hookrightarrow L\otimes L\overset{\lambda_{L}}{\to}\bigoplus_{r,s>0}\mathbf{Q}}
given by the restriction of the
(-1)^{n}
-hyperbolic form, as defined above. This is
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z})
-equivariant as it is the restriction of a
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
-equivariant map
\lambda_{L}
. On the other hand, it is visibly surjective, and the target is infinitely generated, hence so are the coinvariants of the source. By the same argument, this map to
I
is compatible with stabilisation and hence the colimit is also infinitely generated.
∎

Remark 5.5.3
.

The proof of the lemma above can be extended to show that the map of
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces
\smash{\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\right]_{\smash{\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}}}\to I
defined above is an isomorphism for
g\geq 2
. We sketch this computation now, which hinges on the following classical fact: let
G
be a group and
M\to N
be a surjective map of
\mathbf{Z}[G]
-modules where the action of
N
is trivial. If there exists a subgroup
H<G
such that the map
[M]_{H}\to N
is an isomorphism, then so is the map
[M]_{G}\to N
. Consider the subgroup
H
of
\mathrm{U}_{g}(\mathbf{Z},\Lambda_{n}^{\min})
generated by the matrix
M
taking
a_{i}
to
b_{i}
, and
b_{i}
to
(-1)^{n}a_{i}
, and by matrices of the form
P\oplus(P^{-1})^{\vee}
, for
P\in\GL_{n}(\mathbf{Z})
either a permutation matrix or a matrix taking the standard basis element
a_{i}
to
a_{i}+a_{j}
and fixing the remaining basis elements, for some
i\neq j
. Then one can show that the surjective map
\smash{\left[S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L)\right]_{H}}\to I
is injective, by a variation of the proof of
Lemma

5.5.4
below. Applying the classical fact above, one concludes the claim.

The following lemma concerns the reflection involution on
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
.

Lemma 5.5.4
.

For
g\geq 3
, the
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
-coinvariants of the representations:

1.

(H_{Y_{g}})^{\otimes 2}
vanish.

2.

(H_{X_{g}})^{\otimes 2}
are in the
(-1)
-eigenspace of the reflection involution.

Proof.

We start by proving
1
. Let
\{a_{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,g}
be the
\mathbf{Q}[\pi]
-generating set of
H_{Y_{g}}
represented by the inclusions
S^{n}\times\{*\}\subset V_{g}\subset Y_{g}.
Then, as a
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space,
(H_{Y_{g}})^{\otimes 2}
is generated by
t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}
for
k,l\in\mathbf{Z}
and
1\leq i,j\leq g.
We shall show that
t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}
vanishes in the coinvariants for any choice of indices. We use
[x]
to represent the class in the coinvariants represented by
x\in(H_{Y_{g}})^{\otimes 2}
. Let
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq g
be an integer different from
i
and
j
(possible as
g\geq 3
). Consider the matrix
A\in\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
which takes leaves all generators fixed except
a_{i^{\prime}}
which is mapped to
a_{i^{\prime}}+a_{i}.
Then, after taking coinvariants, the following sequence of equalities holds

[t^{k}a_{i^{\prime}}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}]=[A(t^{k}a_{i^{\prime}})\otimes A(t^{l}a_{j})]=[t^{k}(a_{i^{\prime}}+a_{i})\otimes t^{l}a_{j}]=[t^{k}a_{i^{\prime}}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}]+[t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}],

from where conclude that
[t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}]
vanishes. This proves
1
.

We continue with
2
. Let now
\{b_{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,g}
be the classes in
H_{X_{g}}
represented by the inclusion
\{*\}\times S^{n}\subset W_{g,1}\subset X_{g}
, which together with
\{a_{i}\}_{i}
form a
\mathbf{Q}[\pi]
-generating set of
H_{X_{g}}.
Thus, as a
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space
(H_{X_{g}})^{\otimes 2}
is generated by

t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}\quad\qquad t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}b_{j}\quad\qquad t^{k}b_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}\qquad\quad t^{k}b_{i}\otimes t^{l}b_{j}.

The same argument as the paragraph above proves that
[t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}]=0=[t^{k}b_{i}\otimes t^{l}b_{j}].
As the reflection involution acts by
-1
on the generators
a_{i}
and the
+1
on the
b_{i}
, we conclude that it acts by
-1
on
t^{k}a_{i}\otimes t^{l}b_{j}
and
t^{k}b_{i}\otimes t^{l}a_{j}.
This finishes the proof of
2
.
∎

We are now ready to prove
5.5.1
using
Lemmas

5.5.2
and
5.5.4
.

Proof of
5.5.1
.

We start by showing
(i)
. By
4.6.4
and
[
22
, 1677]
, we see that there is an isomorphism
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})\cong\mathcal{L}_{X}
, so it suffices to show that
\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X}
is surjective. As mentioned below
5.5.1
, this map is given by the colimit of the maps (
5.5.1
) after taking appropriate coinvariants, which in turn is an extension of the maps defined in

1

and

2

. The former map is induced by the projection
L^{Y}\to L
and hence is surjective. The latter is surjective (in fact, the projection to a factor) by
5.1.5
. Hence, the induced map after taking coinvariants is a surjection, and so is the map after taking colimits, as colimits commute with cokernels. This establishes
(i)
.

We now turn to prove
(ii)
. To do so, we start by showing that
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\ker(\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X})
are infinitely generated. This claim follows directly from
Lemma

5.5.2
, as the colimits of the systems in loc.cit. receive surjections from
\mathcal{L}_{X}
and
\ker(\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X})
, respectively, coming from taking colimits of the edge morphism
\widetilde{\H}_{2n-2}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim};\mathbf{Q}\right)\to S^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)}(L^{Y})
of the spectral sequence used in
5.4.2
, and similarly for
X_{g}
. Now to prove
(ii)
, observe that
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
is a summand of the source of the map we are interested in, by
5.4.3
, and that
\mathcal{L}_{X}
is isomorphic to the target of this map, by
4.6.4
, as
\smash{\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}((\text{{B}}\check{\Xi}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g})^{+})}
vanishes, by the proof of loc.cit. The claim readily follows.

We finish this proof by concluding from
Lemma

5.5.4
that
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
is in the
(-1)
-eigenspace of the reflection involution, hence establishing
(iii)
. First, notice that this property is closed under subquotients, sums, and filtered colimits. Recall that
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
is the colimit of the
\smash{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
-coinvariants of
\smash{V\coloneq\widetilde{\H}_{2n-2}\left(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}^{\sim};\mathbf{Q}\right)}
. As the action of
\smash{\check{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{sfr},\ell}_{g}}
on the latter vector space factors through
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
, the coinvariants for these two actions agree. By
5.4.2
, the vector space
V
is a sum of a quotient of
(L^{Y})^{\otimes 2}
and a quotient of
N^{Y}
. By proceeding analogously as in the previous two paragraphs, one deduces that the coinvariants of the former are in the
(-1)
-eigenspace, as
L^{Y}
is a sum of
H_{V_{g}}\otimes H_{g}
. Recall from
5.3.2
and
5.1.5
that
N^{Y}
is a sum of a quotient of various quotients of the representations present in
1
and
2
of
Lemma

5.5.4
. Thus, we see that
V
lies in the
(-1)
-eigenspace after taking
\GL_{g}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
-coinvariants, and hence the same holds for
\mathcal{L}_{Y}.
This finishes the proof.
∎

6
Proofs of the main theorems

In this final section, we use the results of the previous sections to establish the main results stated in the introduction, together with some related consequences. Sections
6.1
,
6.2
, and
6.3
depend on one another, while the remaining sections are essentially independent and may be read in any order.

6.1
A
: Even-dimensional solid tori

We now combine the work of
[
22
]
with our computations, in the form of
4.6.4
, to deduce
A
for
d=2n
. We can consider the plus-constructed stably framed “Weiss fibre sequence”

\BDiffsfr(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})_{L}\to\BDiffsfr(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+}\to\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell}

(6.1.1)

defined as in
[
22
, (15) in p.1666]
, with the difference that we take the homotopy colimit over
g
before plus-constructing (see also (
2.1.1
)). Using the work of Galatius and Randal-Williams
[
37
]
, the total space of (
6.1.1
) is weakly equivalent to
\Omega^{\infty}_{0}\Sigma^{\infty-2n}_{+}(S^{1}\times\SO/\SO(2n))
(see also
[
22
, (19) in p.1668]
). We shall use the computation of the rational homotopy groups of the latter in
[
22
, Lemma 7.2.(ii)]
along with
4.6.4
to deduce
A
.

Proof of
A
for
d=2n
.

We start by computing the rational homotopy groups of
\smash{\BDiffsfr(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})_{L}}
. By
[
22
, Lemma 7.2.(ii)]
, we know that
\smash{\pi_{k}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffsfr(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})}
is one-dimensional for
k=1
and vanishes for all
1<k\leq 2n+2.
Moreover, by the discussion in p. 1678 of loc.cit., the induced map
\smash{\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffsfr(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})}\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})
is injective. Hence, we conclude that, for
1\leq*\leq 2n-3

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffsfr(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1});\ell)\cong\pi_{*-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\text{{B}}\mathrm{U}_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi],\Lambda_{n}^{\min})^{+}\right)\oplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathcal{L}_{X}&*=2n-3,\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

by
4.6.4
and the long exact sequence of the fibre sequence (
6.1.1
). Consider now the fibre sequence

\map_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1},\SO/\SO(2n))\to\BDiffsfr(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}).

By
[
22
, 1678]
, the connecting maps in the long exact sequence of this fibre sequence are zero. For
*\leq 2n-4
, we see that the source and target of the injective map

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\map_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1},\SO/\SO(2n)))\to\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffsfr(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))

are finite-dimensional vector spaces with the same dimension, by the computation of the left-hand side in p.1678 of loc.cit. and the computation of
\pi_{*-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\text{{B}}\mathrm{U}_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi],\Lambda_{n}^{\min})^{+}\right)
in Lemma 7.3 of loc.cit (see also
3.3.2
). Hence, this map is an isomorphism. In degree
2n-3
, we see that the left-hand side above vanishes, by the same computation. Thus, we conclude that

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathcal{L}_{X}&*=2n-3,\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(6.1.2)

for all
*\leq 2n-3
. This finishes the proof, as
\mathcal{L}_{X}
is infinite-dimensional by
(ii)
of
5.5.1
.
∎

6.2
B
: Concordances of even-dimensional solid tori

We shall now apply
5.4.3
and
5.5.1
to compute the rational homotopy type of
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
in a range, hence establishing
B
for
d=2n
. More precisely, we establish the following strengthening of
B
, which will be crucial to deduce
C
.

Theorem 6.2.1

(
B
,
d
even)
.

For
n\geq 3
and
1\leq*\leq 2n-3
, then

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\right)\cong\K_{*+1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])\oplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathcal{L}_{Y}&*=2n-3,\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

We proceed analogously as in the last subsection. Recall the Weiss fibre sequence (
2.1.2
) for the triad
Y_{g}

\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g})_{\ell}\to\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell},

which deloops once. By stacking in the
D^{2n-1}
-coordinate, the space
\text{{B}}^{2}\mathrm{C}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
admits the structure of a
\mathbf{E}_{2n-3}
-algebra. In particular, it has abelian fundamental group if
n\geq 3.
Thus, the once delooped fibre sequence is plus-constructible in the sense of
[
7
]
. As in the even-dimensional case, we focus on the plus-constructed and stabilised fibre sequence

\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell}\to\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell}.

(6.2.1)

Work of the first named author, namely by applying
[
63
, Cor. 7.3.3]
, identifies the total space as (the identity component of) the infinite loop space
\Omega^{\infty}_{0}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(S^{1}\times\oo(2n+1)).

Proof of
6.2.1
.

We start by computing the rational homotopy groups of
\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell}\simeq\Omega^{\infty}_{0}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(S^{1}\times\oo(2n+1))
in this range. Since the inclusion
\mathrm{O}(2n+1)\subset\mathrm{O}
is rationally
(4n+2)
-connected, we see that
\pi_{k}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})
is
1
-dimensional for
k=1
and it vanishes for
1<k\leq 4n+2.
By combining the long exact sequence of the fibre sequence (
6.2.1
) with
5.4.3
, we conclude our initial claim for
2\leq*\leq 2n-4.
We continue by showing the case
*=1
, for which we claim that the induced map
\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})
is zero. Since the target of this map is one-dimensional, it suffices to show that the map

\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})\cong\pi_{1}(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+})

(6.2.2)

is non-zero. Observe that post-composing this map with
\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\text{{B}}\mathrm{U}_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi],\Lambda_{n}^{\min})^{+})
agrees with the composite

\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\text{{B}}\mathrm{U}_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi],\Lambda_{n}^{\min})^{+}).

In this composition, the right map is injective by
[
22
, 1678]
. Thus, it suffices to show that the left map is non-trivial to show that this composition, and hence (
6.2.2
), is non-trivial. In loc.cit., the authors construct a non-zero homomorphism

\gamma:\mathbf{Z}\to\pi_{1}(\BDiffsfr(X_{g})_{\ell})

for any
g\geq 1
. We shall show that
\gamma
lifts to
\pi_{1}(\BDiffsfr(Y_{g})_{\ell}).
Let
D^{2n}\subset X_{g}
be an embedded codimension
0
disc disjoint from
\partial^{v}Y_{g}
and let
Y_{g}^{\prime}
denote the triad
(Y_{g},X_{g}\backslash\int(D^{2n}),\partial^{v}Y_{g}\cup D^{2n})
. Let
Y^{\prime}
and
Y
be the homotopy fibres of the maps

\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g-1}^{\prime})_{\ell}\to\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g-1})_{\ell}\qquad\BDiffsfr(X_{g-1}\backslash\int(D^{2n}))_{\ell}\to\BDiffsfr(X_{g-1})_{\ell}

respectively. Comparing stably framed diffeomorphisms with diffeomorphisms, one can check that the map
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is equivalent to the induced map on vertical homotopy fibres of the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Emb((D^{2n+1},D^{2n}),(Y_{g};X_{g}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Emb(D^{2n},X_{g})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun((T^{s}D^{2n+1},T^{s}D^{2n}),(T^{s}Y_{g},T^{s}X_{g}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun(T^{s}D^{2n},T^{s}X_{g})}

where
T^{s}(-)
denotes the stable tangent bundle, and
\Bun(-,-)
denotes the space of stable vector bundle maps (or of arrows of vector bundles). The vertical maps are given by taking stable derivatives. We leave this check to the reader. Additionally, one can check that the horizontal maps are weak equivalences by the uniqueness of collars. Hence, the map
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is also a weak equivalence. In loc.cit., the map
\gamma
is defined as the induced map on fundamental groups of the map
Y\to\BDiffsfr(X_{g-1}\backslash\int(D^{2n}))_{\ell}\to\BDiffsfr(X_{g})_{\ell}
. This map lifts to
\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g};X_{g})_{\ell}
by using the map
Y^{\prime}\to\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g-1}^{\prime})_{\ell}\to\BDiff^{\mathrm{sfr}}_{\partial^{v}}(Y_{g})_{\ell}
and the fact that
Y^{\prime}\to Y
is an isomorphism on fundamental groups, hence establishing the case
*=1
.
∎

From the proofs of
A
and
6.2.1
, we can conclude that the map on the
(2n-3)
-th rational homotopy groups induced by the restriction map
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
is isomorphic to the map in
5.5.1
. In particular, this proves the following corollary.

Corollary 6.2.2
.

For
n\geq 3
, the map

\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))\to\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))

is surjective and has infinitely generated kernel.

6.3
A
and
B
: Odd-dimensional solid tori

In this section, we compute the rational homotopy groups of
\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
and
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
and establish
A
for
d=2n+1
in a range, from
A
for
d=2n
and
5.5.1
and
6.2.1
. Additionally, we obtain
B
for
d=2n+1
as a corollary.

Proof of
A
for
d=2n+1
.

Consider the following fibre sequence

\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\mathrm{BDiff_{\partial}^{ext}}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})

where the base space is the classifying space of the collection of components of those diffeomorphisms of
S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}
that extend to concordances. By the proof of
A
for
d=2n
, the rational homotopy groups of the base vanish in degrees
*\leq 2n-4
and is isomorphic to
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})
in degree
*=2n-3:
this follows since the map
\iota:\mathrm{BDiff_{\partial}^{ext}}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
induces an isomorphism on homotopy groups of degree
*\geq 2
, and an injection on degree
1
. In the latter degree, both groups are abelian, since
\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
and
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
have abelian fundamental groups. We conclude that
\iota
is an injection on rational homotopy groups in degree
1
, and hence an isomorphism since the target vanishes by loc.cit. By
6.2.1
, the rational homotopy group of the total space in degree
2n-3
is isomorphic to
\smash{\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})}.
Moreover, one can check that the right map above in degree
2n-3
is isomorphic to the map in
5.5.1
—that is,
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})\to\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(X_{\infty})_{\ell}^{+})
—which is is a block sum of
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BGL_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])^{+})\to\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\text{{B}}\mathrm{U}_{\infty}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi],\Lambda_{n}^{\min})^{+}\right)\cong 0
(see
[
22
, Lem. 7.3]
) and
\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X}
. Hence, there is an extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\K_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])\\
\oplus\\
\ker(\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}_{X}).\end{array}}

(6.3.1)

The right-hand term in this extension is infinitely generated by
5.5.1
(ii), and hence so is the middle one. We conclude
A
for
d=2n+1
from the long exact sequence on rational homotopy groups of the fibre sequence above.
∎

Following the same strategy one dimension higher, we get the odd-dimensional case of
B
.

Corollary 6.3.1

(
B
,
d
odd)
.

For
n\geq 3
, the map
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
is rationally
(2n-1)
-connected. In particular, for
1\leq*\leq 2n-4
, we have that

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\right)\cong\K_{*+1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])

and that
\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}\left(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\right)
is an infinite-dimensional
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space.

Proof.

Once again, we consider the fibre sequence
\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n+1})\to\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\mathrm{BDiff_{\partial}^{ext}}(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
. By
A
, the fibre is rationally
(2n-2)
-connected, so the map to the base is indeed rationally
(2n-1)
-connected (for both spaces are connected). Moreover,
\mathrm{BDiff_{\partial}^{ext}}(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
is a rational equivalence (by
A
for
d=2n+1
, and the vanishing of
\smash{\K_{2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])}
), showing the claim. The two addendums follow immediately from
A
for
d=2n+1
.
∎

6.4
C
and
E
: Rational concordance stable range

In
6.2.1
and
6.3.1
, we showed that, in a certain range, the rational homotopy groups of
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})
are independent of
d\geq 6
. In this section, we show that not only are these groups abstractly isomorphic, but also that the concordance stabilisation map

\BC(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\BC(S^{1}\times D^{d})

is rationally
(d-4)
-connected but not
(d-3)
-connected, thereby establishing a special case of
C
. The proof combines results of Krannich–Randal-Williams
[
55
]
for
D^{d}
with the Burghelea–Lashof–Rothenberg splitting
[
19
, Section 6]
. From this special case, the general statement of
C
follows from
[
38
, Thm. 4.4]
, and as a byproduct we also obtain
E
.

6.4.1
Burghelea-Lashof-Rothenberg splittings

We start by recalling
[
19
, Section 6]
for
M=D^{d-1}
. Consider the fibre sequence

\operatorname{C}(D^{d})\to\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\CEmb^{\cong}(D^{d-1},S^{1}\times D^{d-1})

where the
(-)^{\cong}
denotes the path components hit by the right map. This sequence splits using the inclusion
S^{1}\times D^{d-1}\hookrightarrow D^{d}
for
d\geq 2
. Moreover, this sequence is compatible with concordance stabilisation. We have a map
\lambda_{d}:\CEmb(D^{d-1},S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\CEmb(D^{d-1},\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1})
given by lifting embeddings into the universal cover
\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1}
, which is well defined since our embeddings are fixed on
\partial D^{d-1}
which is non-empty if
d\geq 2.
We record here the important properties of these spaces established in
[
19
, Section 6]
.

(i)

The inclusion
\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1}\hookrightarrow S^{1}\times D^{d-1}
induces a splitting of the sequence

\hofib_{\iota}(\lambda_{d})\to\CEmb(D^{d-1},S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\CEmb(D^{d-1},\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1})

after taking loop spaces. This is
[
19
, Lemma 2, p.103]
.

(ii)

The fibre sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}([-1,0]\times D^{d-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CEmb_{\{-1\}\times D^{d-1}\cup[-1,0]\times\partial D^{d-1}}([-1,0]\times D^{d-1},\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CEmb(\{0\}\times D^{d-1},\mathbf{R}\times D^{d-1})_{\iota}}

where the subscript
\iota
denotes the component of the inclusion, deloops once and has contractible total space. This is
[
19
, Lemma 2]
. Moreover, this delooping is compatible with the concordance stabilisation maps.

From the last property, we obtain a map

\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\operatorname{C}(D^{d})\times\BC(D^{d})

(6.4.1)

which is compatible with concordance stabilisation and whose fibre identifies with
\hofib_{\iota}(\lambda_{d}).
The next lemma shows that this fibre has vanishing rational homotopy groups.

Proposition 6.4.1
.

If
d\geq 6
, the map (
6.4.1
) induces an isomorphism on rational homotopy groups of degree
1\leq i\leq d-6
. Moreover, if
d
is even, then the same claim holds additionally for
i=d-5.

Proof.

By
[
19
, Thm.2, p.104]
, this map is surjective on homotopy groups in degrees
i\geq 1
. We shall compute the rational homotopy groups on each side and show they have the same (finite) dimension, hence proving that it induces an isomorphism on rational homotopy groups. By
6.2.1
and
6.3.1
, we have
\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1}))
is isomorphic to
\K_{i+2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi]).
On the other hand, Krannich computed the rational homotopy groups of the right-hand side in this range. By
[
57
, Thm. A]
, the group
\smash{\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(D^{d}))}
is isomorphic to
\smash{\K_{i+1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}))}.
Thus, the
i
-th rational homotopy group of the right-hand side is

\K_{i+1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}))\oplus\K_{i+2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})
, which has the same dimension as
\K_{i+2}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])
by the Bass–Heller–Swan’s theorem.
∎

6.4.2
Proofs of
C
and
E

Proposition 6.4.2
.

For
d\geq 6
, then the concordance stabilisation map

\BC(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\BC(S^{1}\times D^{d})

is rationally
(d-4)
-connected, but not rationally
(d-3)
-connected. Moreover, the
(d-3)
-rd relative rational homotopy group of this map is infinite-dimensional.

Proof.

We first show that this map is rationally
(d-4)
-connected. Since source and target are connected, it suffices to argue that it is rationally
(d-5)
-connected upon looping once. Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}(D^{d})\times\BC(D^{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{C}(D^{d+1})\times\BC(D^{d+1}).}
(
6.4.1
)
(
6.4.1
)

The right vertical map is rationally
(d-4)
-connected by
[
55
, Cor. B]
. If
d
is even, then both horizontal maps induce isomorphisms on rational homotopy groups in degrees
i\leq d-5
by
6.4.1
, hence establishing that the left map is
(d-5)
-connected. If
d
is odd, then the top map induces an isomorphism in degrees
i\leq d-6
(and a surjection in all positive degrees) and the bottom map induces an isomorphism in degrees
i\leq d-5
, again by loc.cit. Hence, the left vertical map is a surjection in degree
d-5
.

To show that the map in the claim is not
(d-3)
-connected, we must treat separately the cases
d
even and
d
odd. When
d
is odd, since by the previous argument the map in question is at least
(d-4)
-connected, it suffices to prove that the induced map on
\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}
is not an isomorphism. However, by
6.2.1
and
6.3.1
, we check that the source is infinite-dimensional, while the target is finite-dimensional. For the second claim, we see that the relative rational homotopy group in degree
d-3
surjects onto the kernel of the induced map on
\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}
, which is infinitely generated.

If
d=2n
is even, we shall show that the stabilisation map

\Sigma:\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))\longrightarrow\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))

is not surjective. Recall that there are two relevant involutions in the spaces
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
and
\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n})
; the concordance involution
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
and the reflection involution
\rho
. Let us record how
\Sigma
and the groups involved in this map interact with these two involutions:

(i)

For any manifold
M
, the stabilisation map
\pi_{*}(\BC(M))\to\pi_{*}(\BC(M\times I))
is anti-equivariant with respect to the concordance involution
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}

[
42
, Appendix I, Lemma]
.

(ii)

The restriction map
\operatorname{C}(M)\to\Diff_{\partial}(M)
is equivariant for
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
in domain and inversion on the target.

(iii)

In a topological group
G
, the inversion map
\mathrm{inv}:G\to G
acts by
-1
on homotopy groups.

(iv)

The natural map
\BDiff_{\partial}(M\times[0,1])\to\BC(M)
is equivariant for the reflection involution
\rho_{[0,1]}
on the source and the concordance involution
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
on the target. If, moreover,
M=N\times I
for some manifold
N
, then the reflection involutions
\rho_{I}
and
\rho_{[0,1]}
on
N\times I\times[0,1]
are homotopic, and thus induce the same involution on homotopy groups.

Now observe that the restricion map

\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))\longrightarrow\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))

is an isomorphism since the groups
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n+1}))
are zero for
*\leq 2n-2
by
A
for
d=2n+2
. Thus, by
(ii)
and
(iii)
, it follows that
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
acts by
-1
on
\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))
, the target of
\Sigma
. Since this
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space is infinite-dimensional by
A
for
d=2n+1
, it follows from
(i)
that it suffices to show that the
(+1)
-eigenspace of
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
on
\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))
, the source of
\Sigma
, is finite-dimensional—in fact, we will show that it is at most
1
-dimensional, hence also establishing the second claim.

To this end, consider the extension obtained from the standard fibre sequence
\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})
by applying
\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(-)
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}_{X},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
I\coloneq\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))
and
K\coloneq\K_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{2n-3}(\mathbf{Z})
; see
6.2.1
and (
6.1.2
). This is an extension of
\mathbf{Q}[\langle\rho,\iota_{\operatorname{C}}\rangle]
-modules, and we need to understand the effect of
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
on the middle term—let us write
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right):K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}\to K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}
in matrix form. We claim that
D=-\Id_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}
and that either
B=0
or
C=0
. In particular, since
K
is at most
1
-dimensional, it will follow from this that the
(+1)
-eigenspace of
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
on
K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}
is at most
1
-dimensional, as we wish to show.

So let
(0,x)\in K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}
and write
(b,\overline{x})\coloneq\iota_{\operatorname{C}}(0,x)
, i.e.
b=B(x)
and
\overline{x}=D(x)
. Since
p
is equivariant for
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
on the source and
-1
on the target, it follows that
(0,x)+\iota_{\operatorname{C}}(0,x)\in\ker p=\im\alpha
, so let
i\in I
be such that
\alpha(i)=(0,x)+\iota_{\operatorname{C}}(0,x)
. Note that
\alpha(i)
is in the invariant subspace of
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}
, and thus

(b,x+\overline{x})=\alpha(i)=\iota_{\operatorname{C}}\alpha(i)=\alpha(\iota_{\operatorname{C}}(i))=\alpha(\rho(i))=\rho(\alpha(i)),

where the second to last equality follows from
(iv)
. But observe that, as a
\langle\rho\rangle
-representation,
K\oplus\mathcal{L}_{Y}
really is split and
\rho
acts by
-1
on
\mathcal{L}_{Y}
: indeed, this follows from
5.4.3
,
5.5.1
(iii) and the fact that the map
\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\BEmb_{\partial_{0}}^{\mathrm{sfr},{\cong}}}(Y_{\infty};X_{\infty})^{+}_{\ell})\to\pi_{2n-3}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1}))
is a
\langle\rho\rangle
-equivariant isomorphism. Hence, we obtain that
(b,x+\overline{x})=(\rho(b),-x-\overline{x})
, which yields
\overline{x}=-x
. This shows that
D=-\Id_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}
. But from
\iota_{\operatorname{C}}^{2}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A^{2}+BC&AB-B\\
CA-C&CB+\Id_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\Id_{K}&0\\
0&\Id_{\mathcal{L}_{Y}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, we get that
CB=0
. Since
\dim K=\dim(\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{2n-3}(\mathbf{Z}))\leq 1
, it follows that either
B=0
or
C=0
, as desired. This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.4.3
.

Note that
\smash{\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))\to\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n+1}))}
is surjective for
2n\geq 6
: by
6.2.1
, we have
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n+1}))\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{2n-1}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])\cong\K_{2n-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})
, where the second isomorphism follows from the Bass–Heller–Swan decomposition and the well-known fact that
\K_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})=0
for
*\not\equiv 1\mod 4
(and a copy of
\mathbf{Q}
otherwise). Thus, since
\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(D^{2n+2}))\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{2n-1}(\mathbf{Z})
by
[
55
, Cor. B]
, the right-hand vertical map in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(S^{1}\times D^{2n+1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(D^{2n+1}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2n-2}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BC(D^{2n+2}))}

must be isomorphism by degree reasons since it is a split epimorphism. The bottom horizontal map is also surjective by loc.cit., so it follows that the top map is surjective, as claimed.

Proof of
C
.

The result follows from
6.4.2
and applying
[
38
, Thm. 4.4 and Ex. 4.3]
for
k=2
and
N=S^{1}\times D^{d-1}
.
∎

Proof of
E
.

Since the space
\operatorname{C}(N)
is path-connected as
N
is simply-connected (by Cerf’s
pseudo-isotopy implies isotopy
theorem
[
26
]
), we have a map of fibre sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CEmb(\nu M,N)_{\iota}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BC(N-\nu M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BC(N)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\CEmb(\nu M\times[0,1],N\times[0,1])_{\iota}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BC((N-\nu M)\times[0,1])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\BC(N\times[0,1]),}

where the vertical maps are the concordance stabilisation maps. By
[
38
, Cor. C]
, the right-hand vertical map is rationally
(d-3)
-connected and the
(d-3)
-th rational homotopy group of its fibre is finite-dimensional. By our hypothesis,
N-\nu M
is spin and its fundamental group is isomorphic to
\mathbf{Z}
, so by
C
, the middle vertical map is rationally
(d-4)
-connected and the
(d-4)
-th rational homotopy group of its fibre is infinite-dimensional. Thus, by the long exact sequences of both fibre sequences, together with the
4
-lemma, we obtain that the left vertical map is rationally
(d-4)
-connected and that the
(d-4)
-th rational homotopy group of the fibre is infinite-dimensional. To finish the proof, observe that the restriction map
\CEmb(\nu M,N)_{\iota}\to\CEmb(M,N)_{\iota}
is a weak equivalence; see e.g.
[
66
, Prop. 3.3]
.
∎

6.5
D
: Upper bound on the rational concordance stable range

We will now use
C
to deduce
D
. We will proceed by the following strategy, which inspired by
[
38
, Corollaries B and C]
. We thank Manuel Krannich for outlining to us this idea. Consider the general setting of a chain of inclusions of smooth compact spin
d
-dimensional manifolds
N\subset M\subset N^{\prime}
and suppose that the induced maps
\pi_{1}(N)\to\pi_{1}(N^{\prime})
is an isomorphism, then we conclude by Theorem 4.4 (ii) of loc.cit that the composition

\frac{\operatorname{C}(N\times I)}{\operatorname{C}(N)}\to\frac{\operatorname{C}(M\times I)}{\operatorname{C}(M)}\to\frac{\operatorname{C}(N^{\prime}\times I)}{\operatorname{C}(N^{\prime})}

is an equivalence on Postnikov
(d-3)
-truncations, where
\frac{\operatorname{C}(N\times I)}{\operatorname{C}(N)}
denotes the homotopy fibre of the map
\BC(N)\to\BC(N\times I)
. In particular, we conclude that the map

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(N\times I),\operatorname{C}(N))\to\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(M\times I),\operatorname{C}(M))

is injective for
*\leq d-3.
We will use this strategy for
N=S^{1}\times D^{d-1}
.

Proof of
D
.

Let
M
be a
d
-dimensional compact spin manifold such that
\H^{1}(\pi_{1}(M),\mathbf{Q})\neq 0
. Let
G
denote
\pi_{1}(M)
, which is finitely generated since
M
is compact. Observe that, by
[
38
, Theorem 4.4 (ii)]
, the group
\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(M\times I),\operatorname{C}(M))
is of the same isomorphism type as the same group where
M
is replaced by any other compact spin manifold with fundamental group
G
, so it suffices to prove the claim in the corollary for any such manifold. Choose a handle decomposition of
M
relative to its boundary, and let
M^{\prime}
be the union of all handles of index at least
3
. Observe that the inclusion
\partial M^{\prime}\hookrightarrow M
is
2
-connected (since it is equivalent to a relative CW complex of cells of dimensions at least
3
) and that the inclusion
M^{\prime}\hookrightarrow M
is
(d-3)
-connected (by reversing the handle structure). Since
d\geq 6
, we see that these inclusions are fundamental groupoid isomorphisms. Thus, by replacing
M
with
M^{\prime}
(which is also spin by restriction), we can assume that
\partial M\hookrightarrow M
is a fundamental groupoid isomorphism.

We will now construct the chain of submanifolds indicated above. Since
\H^{1}(G;\mathbf{Q})
does not vanish and
G=\pi_{1}(M)
is finitely generated, there exists a surjective map
p:G\to\mathbf{Z}
. Pick an element of
G
which maps to
1
under
p
and represent it by an embedding
S^{1}\hookrightarrow M^{\circ}
, which, since
M
is spin and thus orientable, has trivial normal bundle and can be extended to an embedding of
S^{1}\times D^{d-1}.
The image of this embedding will play the role of
N
above. We now construct
N^{\prime}
. Let
K
denote the kernel of
p
. A standard fact in group theory shows that
K
is normally finitely generated as a subgroup of
G
—that is, it is the normal closure of finitely many elements of
K
in
G
—since
G
is finitely generated and
\mathbf{Z}
is finitely presented. Pick finitely many elements
\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}
generating this module and realise them as embeddings
s_{i}^{\prime}:S^{1}\times D^{2n-2}\hookrightarrow\partial M
, which is possible since
n\geq 6
,
\partial M\hookrightarrow M
is a fundamental groupoid isomorphism, and
\partial M
is orientable. Let
N^{\prime}
be the manifold obtained from
M
by attaching
2
-handles along the embeddings
s_{i}^{\prime}
. Since attaching a cell kills the normal closure of the attaching map on
\pi_{1}
, we see that
\pi_{1}(N^{\prime})\cong G/K\cong\mathbf{Z}
, and the map
\pi_{1}(N)\to\pi_{1}(N^{\prime})
is an isomorphism. This manifold is spin, since
\mathrm{BSpin}(d)
is
1
-connected. Hence, we can run the argument described above to conclude that the map

\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d}),\operatorname{C}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1}))\to\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\operatorname{C}(M\times I),\operatorname{C}(M))

is injective, and hence the target is infinite-dimensional, as the source is by
C
.
∎

6.6
Homeomorphisms of solid tori

We deduce a computation of the rational homotopy groups of the classifying space
\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{d})
of homeomorphisms of solid tori using the already mentioned work of Burghelea–Lashof–Rothenberg.

Theorem 6.6.1
.

For
d\geq 6
and
*<d-3
if
d
is even and
*<d-4
if
d
is odd, then

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\big)=0.

Moreover, this vector space is infinite-dimensional if
*=d-3
and
d
is even, or if
*=d-4
and
d
is odd.

Proof.

We start by showing this statement for even
d=2n
. As in
[
22
, Section 7.7]
, smoothing theory gives a fibre sequence

\Map_{\partial}\big(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1},\tfrac{\Top(2n)}{\mathrm{O}(2n)}\big)\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})\to\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n-1})

and the fibre is rationally
(2n-2)
-connected. The result follows from
A
for
d=2n
. We move now to
d=2n+1
. We first prove the case
k=1
. Taking the long exact sequence of the analogous smoothing theory fibre sequence for
d=2n+1
, we have

\cdots\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\pi_{0}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\Map_{\partial}\big(S^{1}\times D^{2n},\tfrac{\Top(2n+1)}{\mathrm{O}(2n+1)}\big)\big)\to\cdots.

Upon rationalising, the leftmost group is isomorphic mto
\K_{2}(\mathbf{Z}[\pi])\cong\K_{1}(\mathbf{Z})\oplus\K_{2}(\mathbf{Z})
. This group is finite and thus its rationalisation is trivial. For the rightmost group, first note that there is a natural equivalence
\Map_{\partial}(Y\times D^{d-1},X)\simeq\Omega^{d-1}\Map(Y,X)
, and since the fibre sequence
\Omega X\to\Map(S^{1},X)\to X
admits a section (the constant loops), we get that for any space
X
,

\pi_{k}(\Map_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n},X))\cong\pi_{k+2n}(X)\oplus\pi_{k+2n+1}(X).

When
X=\Top(2n+1)/\mathrm{O}(2n+1)
, the homotopy groups
\pi_{*}(X)
are finite for
*\leq 2n+2
by
[
52
, Essay V, 5.0]
. Thus, we see that the rationalisation for
k=0
is trivial, hence proving that
\pi_{1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))
vanishes. We focus now on the case
k>1
. Let
\lambda:\Emb_{\partial}(D^{2n},S^{1}\times D^{2n})\to\Emb_{\partial}(D^{2n},\mathbf{R}\times D^{2n})
given by lifting embeddings to the universal cover (this is analogous to
\lambda_{d}
from the previous section). Since
\Top_{\partial}(D^{2n})
is contractible by the Alexander trick, we deduce from
[
19
, Thm. 2 for
\Top
,p.104]
and
[
19
, Thm. 3, p.104]
that
\pi_{i}(\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))
is abstractly isomorphic to
\pi_{i-1}(\hofib(\lambda))
for
i\geq 2
. On the other hand,
[
19
, Thm.2, p.104]
for the smooth category, we have a splitting

\pi_{i}(\Diffb(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))\cong\pi_{i}(\Diffb(D^{2n+1}))\oplus\pi_{i-1}(\Diffb(D^{2n+1}))\oplus\pi_{i}(\hofib(\lambda))

for all
i\geq 1
. In this range,
[
55
, Thm. A]
implies that
\pi^{\mathbf{Q}}_{i}(\Diff_{\partial}(D^{2n+1}))\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{i+2}(\mathbf{Z})
. By applying
A
for
d=2n+1
, together with dimension count as in the proof of
6.4.1
, we see that
\pi^{\mathbf{Q}}_{i}(\hofib(\lambda))
vanishes for
i\leq 2n-5
and is infinitely generated in
i=2n-4
. We conclude that
\smash{\pi^{\mathbf{Q}}_{i}(\BTop_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{2n}))}
vanishes for
i\leq 2n-4
and is infinitely generated for
i=2n-3
.
∎

6.7
F
: Diffeomorphisms of
S^{1}\times N
for
N
simply-connected spin

As an application of
C
, we now obtain
F
. Let
N^{d-1}
be as in the statement of this corollary, i.e. smooth compact simply-connected spin of dimension
d-1\geq 5
(and possibly with boundary). Our approach to study the groups
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times N))
is via the classical surgery-pseudoisotopy fibre sequence

\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N)\longrightarrow\BDiffb(S^{1}\times N)\longrightarrow\BbDiffb(S^{1}\times N).

(6.7.1)

By
[
16
, Cor. F(2)]
, this fibre sequence splits upon looping twice, truncating in the concordance stable range, and localising at odd primes (in particular, rationalising), so it suffices to study the base and fibre independently. The block term is completely described in terms of
\bDiffb(N)
, by the following Bass–Heller–Swan-like decomposition of Burghelea
[
17
, Cor. 2.3]
.

Lemma 6.7.1

(Burghelea)
.

Let
N
be a compact connected smooth manifold with
\mathrm{Wh}_{1}(\pi_{1}N)=0
, and let
T
be
S^{1}
if
\partial N=\emptyset
, and a singleton otherwise. Then, there is an equivalence
\bDiffb(S^{1}\times N)\simeq\bDiffb(N)\times\Omega\bDiffb(N)\times T
.

We now focus on the pseudoisotopy term. We will need the following computation.

Lemma 6.7.2
.

Let
X
be a based simply-connected space. There is an isomorphism

\H_{*}^{S^{1}}(L(X\times S^{1}),LS^{1};\mathbf{Q})\cong\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LX;\mathbf{Q}).

Under this isomorphism, the split monomorphism (cf.
Equation

4.1.22
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\H_{*+1}(X\times S^{1},S^{1};\mathbf{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HC}^{-}_{*+1}(\mathbf{S}[\Omega(X\times S^{1})],\mathbf{S}[\Omega S^{1}];\mathbf{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\H^{S^{1}}_{*}(L(X\times S^{1}),LS^{1};\mathbf{Q})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

only hits the
(r=0)
-summand. Thus, we obtain an isomorphism

\frac{\H^{S^{1}}_{*}(L(X\times S^{1}),LS^{1};\mathbf{Q})}{\H_{*+1}(X\times S^{1},S^{1};\mathbf{Q})}\cong\frac{\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LX;\mathbf{Q})}{\widetilde{\H}_{*}(X;\mathbf{Q})\oplus\widetilde{\H}_{*+1}(X;\mathbf{Q})}\oplus\bigoplus_{r\neq 0}\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LX;\mathbf{Q}).

Upon taking coinvariants with respect to the involution
(-1)^{d+1}\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}
, we obtain an isomorphism

\left[\frac{\H^{S^{1}}_{*}(L(X\times S^{1}),LS^{1};\mathbf{Q})}{\H_{*+1}(X\times S^{1},S^{1};\mathbf{Q})}\right]_{(-1)^{d+1}\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}}\cong\left[\frac{\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LX;\mathbf{Q})}{\widetilde{\H}_{*}(X;\mathbf{Q})\oplus\widetilde{\H}_{*+1}(X;\mathbf{Q})}\right]_{(-1)^{d+1}\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{r>0}\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LX;\mathbf{Q}).

Proof.

Observe that there is an
S^{1}
-equivariant equivalence
L(S^{1}\times X)=LS^{1}\times LX\simeq\coprod_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}S^{1}\times LX
where the action on the right-hand side preserves the coproduct decomposition and is diagonal on each
S^{1}\times LX
. Since the
S^{1}
-action on
S^{1}
is free, it follows that
(S^{1}\times LX)_{hS^{1}}\simeq LX
, and hence the first claim follows from

\left(\Sigma^{\infty}(L(X\times S^{1})/LS^{1})\right)_{hS^{1}}\simeq\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(S^{1}\times LX))_{hS^{1}}/(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}S^{1})_{hS^{1}}\simeq\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}\Sigma^{\infty}LX.

To see the second claim, observe that the map
c:\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}Y\to(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}LY)^{hS^{1}}
naturally factors through
(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}L_{0}Y)^{hS^{1}}
, where
L_{0}Y\subset LY
is the component of the free loop space corresponding to null-homotopic loops. Also, note that
L_{0}S^{1}\simeq S^{1}
. Thus, we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty}(S^{1}\times X)/S^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma^{\infty}L_{0}(S^{1}\times X)/L_{0}S^{1})^{hS^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma^{\infty}L(S^{1}\times X)/LS^{1})^{hS^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma^{\infty+1}L_{0}(S^{1}\times X)/L_{0}S^{1})_{hS^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma^{\infty+1}L(S^{1}\times X)/LS^{1})_{hS^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{\infty+1}LX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}\Sigma^{\infty+1}LX}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

\scriptstyle\sim

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r=0}

\simeq

\simeq

where both of the vertical arrows are
S^{1}
-norm maps. Both of these are equivalences, for the
S^{1}
-spectra
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}L_{0}(S^{1}\times X)\simeq(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}S^{1})\otimes\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}LX
and
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}L(S^{1}\times X)\simeq(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}S^{1})\otimes(\oplus_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}LX)
are equivalent to induced
S^{1}
-spectra
[
53
, Thm. D]
(same applies when replacing
S^{1}\times X
with
S^{1}
, and hence the cofibre thereof appearing in the above diagram). The bottom equivalences implicitly use that
X
is simply-connected so that
L_{0}X=LX
. The second isomorphism now follows.

Finally, to obtain the last isomorphism on coinvariants, simply observe that
\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}
descends to an involution on the quotient in the right-hand side of the second isomorphism in the statement by
4.1.5
, and, for
r>0
, identifies the
r
and
-r
summands in
L(S^{1}\times X)=\coprod_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}L_{r}(X\times S^{1})=\coprod_{r\in\mathbf{Z}}S^{1}\times LX
.
∎

Lemma 6.7.3
.

Let
N
be a smooth compact spin manifold of dimension
d-1\geq 5
, and let

A_{*}\coloneq\left[\frac{\H_{*}(LN;\mathbf{Q})}{\H_{*}(N;\mathbf{Q})\oplus\H_{*+1}(N;\mathbf{Q})}\right]_{(-1)^{d+1}\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{r>0}\H_{*}(LN;\mathbf{Q}),

where the term inside the coinvariants is the one featuring in
Lemma

6.7.2
. Then, there is an isomorphism in degrees
1\leq*\leq d-5

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N)\big)\cong A_{*}\oplus\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\big),

and an epimorphism in degree
*=d-4

\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N)\big)\twoheadrightarrow A_{*}\oplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{if $d$ even,}\\
\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{d-3}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])&\text{if $d$ odd.}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Consider the Weiss–Williams map (cf. (
4.1.1
))

\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N)\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N)_{hC_{2}}).

By
C
, the rational concordance stable range for
S^{1}\times N
is
d-5
, and hence this map is rationally
(d-4)
-connected. We now compute the rational homotopy groups of the target of this map.

Picking an embedded disc
D^{d-1}\subset N
, we obtain a map of
C_{2}
-spectra
\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N)
(recall the definition of
\mathbf{H}(-)
from
Section

4.1.1
). This map is split injective on homotopy groups, for both spectra are connective and on infinite loop spaces it is the map
\mathcal{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\mathcal{H}(S^{1}\times N)
, which is split injective as
\mathcal{H}(-)\simeq\Omega\Wh(-)
is a homotopy functor. (A more involved argument using
[
66
, Lem. 5.15]
in fact shows that the original map of spectra is split, but we will not need this.) Hence, writing
\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N,S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\coloneq\hocofib(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N))
, we see that there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})_{hC_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}\big(\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})_{hC_{2}})\oplus\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N,S^{1}\times D^{d-1})_{hC_{2}})}

where the right-hand vertical map is the inclusion of the first summand, and where the horizontal maps are isomorphisms in degrees in degrees
*\leq d-5
, and surjective in degree
*=d-4
.

For
*\geq 1
, there is an isomorphism
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1}))\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{*+1}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])
and, by
[
66
, Thm. 5.13]
, the left
C_{2}
-action translates to the involution
(-1)^{d}\tau_{\epsilon}
on the right, where
\tau_{\epsilon}
is the canonical involution on algebraic
K
-theory. Under the Bass–Heller–Swan isomorphism
\smash{\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{*+1}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])\cong\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{*+1}(\mathbf{Z})\oplus\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{*}(\mathbf{Z})}
, the canonical involution acts diagonally on the right-hand side (see e.g.
[
47
]
), and
\tau_{\epsilon}
acts by
-1
on
\K_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})
. We see that for
*\geq 1
,
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})_{hC_{2}})
is zero if
d
is even, and isomorphic to
\K^{\mathbf{Q}}_{*+1}(\mathbf{Z}[\mathbf{Z}])
if
d
is odd.

Hence, it remains to identify
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N,S^{1}\times D^{d-1})_{hC_{2}})
as
A_{*}
. By definition (recall
Equation

4.1.8
from
Section

4.1.1
), there is an equivalence of
C_{2}
-spectra
\mathbf{H}(S^{1}\times N,S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\simeq\Sigma\mathbf{CE}(S^{1}\times(N-D^{d-1}),S^{1}\times N)
, and by
4.1.5
and
Lemma

6.7.2
, the rationaly homotopy groups
\pi_{*-1}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{CE}(S^{1}\times(N-D^{d-1}),S^{1}\times N)_{hC_{2}})
are indeed isomorphic to
A_{*}
, as desired.
∎

Proof of
F
.

By
[
16
, Cor. F(2)]
14
14

14

In the statement loc.cit.,
\omega(n)
stands for a number such that
s_{M}:\operatorname{C}(M)\to\operatorname{C}(M\times I)
is an isomorphism on Postnikov truncations
\tau_{\leq\omega(n)}(-)
for all compact
n
-manifolds
M
; see page 20 loc.cit. By working with a fixed manifold
M=N\times S^{1}
and over the rationals, we can choose
\omega(n)=\phi^{\mathbf{Q}}(S^{1}\times N)-1
.
, the fibre sequence (
6.7.1
) splits in the sense that

\tau_{\leq\phi-1}\big(\Omega^{2}\BDiffb(S^{1}\times N)\big)\simeq_{\mathbf{Q}}\tau_{\leq\phi-1}\big(\Omega^{2}\BbDiffb(S^{1}\times N)\times\Omega^{2}\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times N)\big),

where
\phi\coloneq\phi^{\mathbf{Q}}(S^{1}\times N)=d-5
, by
C
. Thus, the first part of the statement follows by Lemmas
6.7.1
and
6.7.3
, and the observation that the map
\big(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}/\Diff\big)_{\partial}(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})
is a rational weak equivalence since
\BbDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})
is rationally contractible—this can be seen from
Lemma

6.7.1
and the well-known isomorphism
\pi_{*}(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}_{\partial}(D^{d}))\cong\Theta_{d+*+1}
for
d+*\geq 5
, where
\Theta_{n}
is the group of exotic
n
-spheres, which is finite abelian, hence rationally trivial (see
[
50
, Thms 1.1/2]
).

The homotopy groups
\pi_{*}(\bDiffb(N))
are finitely generated for
N
simply-connected (cf.
[
61
, Prop. 5.22(ii)]
), so the addendum follows from Lemmas
6.7.1
and
6.7.3
, and the above splitting.
∎

We now discuss two examples and a corollary to showcase the computational strength of
F
.

Example 6.7.4

(
N=\mathbf{CP}^{n}
)
.

Let
n\geq 3
. We now compute
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times N))
in degrees
1\leq*\leq d-5
using
F
. (By
1.3.2
, the case
*=1
is also covered since
\pi_{0}(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))
, and hence
\pi_{1}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times N))
, is abelian, so can be rationalised: to see this, note that the surgery exact sequence gives an extension
\smash{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))\to\pi_{0}(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))\to\pi_{0}(\Aut(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))}
, where
\smash{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))}
is abelian, e.g. as a consequence of the
h
-cobordism theorem. Noticing that
[\mathbf{CP}^{n},\mathbf{CP}^{n}]\cong[\mathbf{CP}^{n},\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}]\cong\mathbf{Z}
, it easily follows that
\pi_{0}(\Aut(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))\cong\mathbf{Z}/2
, generated by complex conjugation. Thus, the previous sequence is split.) To do so, it suffices to determine (i)
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\bDiffb(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))
, (ii)
\H_{*}(L\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})
and (iii) the
-\tau_{\mathrm{FLS}}
-coinvariants of the cokernel of the homomorphism
\widetilde{\H}_{*}(\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})\oplus\widetilde{\H}_{*+1}(\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\H}_{*}(L\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})
, all in degrees
0\leq*\leq 2n-4
.

Item (i) is computed by first understanding
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\Aut(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))
, either by the Federer spectral sequence or by rational homotopy theory, and later computing the rational homotopy groups of the surgery structure space of
\mathbf{CP}^{n}
by means of surgery theory. Both of these can be done by hand (alternatively, see
[
18
, Cor. 2.E]
), and one obtains that for
1\leq*\leq 2n-1
,

\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}(\mathbf{CP}^{n}))\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathbf{Q}&\text{if $*=1,2$ or $3\leq*\leq 2n$ odd,}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.\oplus\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq n-1,\\
i\equiv k\ \mathrm{mod}\ 2\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Q}&\text{if $*=2k-1\geq 0$ odd,}\\[4.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(The first summand is the rational homotopy of
\Aut(\mathbf{CP}^{n})
, and the second the reduced
KO
-theory of
\mathbf{CP}^{n}
.)

As for (ii), we claim that
\H_{*}(L\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})\cong\mathbf{Q}
in each degree
*\leq 2n-3
. Note that the natural inclusion map
\mathbf{CP}^{n}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}\simeq\text{{B}}S^{1}
is
(2n+1)
-connected, so
L\mathbf{CP}^{n}\to L\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}
is
2n
-connected. Thus, we show the claim for
L\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}
. But the free loop space fibration
S^{1}\simeq\Omega\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}\to L\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}\to\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}
is trivial since
\mathbf{CP}^{\infty}\simeq\text{{B}}S^{1}
is a group. Hence, the claim follows from the Künneth isomorphism

\H_{*}(L\mathbf{CP}^{\infty};\mathbf{Q})\cong\H_{*}(S^{1};\mathbf{Q})\otimes\H_{*}(\mathbf{CP}^{\infty};\mathbf{Q})\cong\H_{*}(\mathbf{CP}^{\infty};\mathbf{Q})\oplus\H_{*+1}(\mathbf{CP}^{\infty};\mathbf{Q}).

Finally, note that term (iii) vanishes since, by the computation above, the monomorphism
\widetilde{\H}_{*}(\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})\oplus\widetilde{\H}_{*+1}(\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\H}_{*}(L\mathbf{CP}^{n};\mathbf{Q})
must be an isomorphism for
*\leq 2n-3
by dimension reasons.

In particular, for every
1\leq*\leq 2n-3
, the
\mathbf{Q}
-vector space
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times\mathbf{CP}^{n}))
is infinite-dimensional.

Example 6.7.5

(
N=\Sigma^{d-1}
a
2
-connected rational homology sphere)
.

Let
d\geq 6
, and let
\Sigma
be a closed
2
-connected
(d-1)
-manifold with
\H_{*}(\Sigma;\mathbf{Q})\cong\H_{*}(S^{d-1};\mathbf{Q})
. Since
\Sigma
has the rational homotopy type of
S^{d-1}
, it follows from
F
that the rational homotopy groups
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times\Sigma))
are finite-dimensional for
2\leq*\leq d-5
(and for
*=1
when
\Sigma
is an actual homotopy sphere by
1.3.2
).

Even though
\H_{d-4}(L\Sigma;\mathbf{Q})=0
, we claim that
\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times\Sigma))
is still infinite-dimensional: write
\Sigma^{\circ}
for the complement of an open disc in
\Sigma
. By handle trading
[
74
, Thm. 3]
, it follows that the handle dimension of
S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\circ}
is
\leq d-3
. Hence, the homotopy type of the fibre in the fibre sequence

\Emb^{\cong}(S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\circ},S^{1}\times\Sigma)\to\BDiffb(S^{1}\times D^{d-1})\to\BDiff(S^{1}\times\Sigma)

(6.7.2)

can be analysed in degrees
*\leq d-3
by means of the embedding surgery-pseudoisotopy approach outlined in
Section

4.1.1
. The block embedding space
\smash{\widetilde{\Emb}}(S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\circ},S^{1}\times\Sigma)
is easily seen to have finitely generated homotopy groups in positive degrees by the block analogue of (
6.7.2
),
Lemma

6.7.1
and the fact that
\smash{\mathrm{\widetilde{\Diff}}}_{\partial}(M)
has finitely generated homotopy groups in positive degrees if
M^{d-1}
is
1
-connected by
[
61
, Prop. 5.22]
. In turn, the pseudoisotopy embedding space
\smash{\Emb^{(\sim)}}(S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\circ},S^{1}\times\Sigma)
is rationally
(d-4)
-connected, since the same holds for the spectrum
\mathbf{CE}(S^{1}\times\Sigma^{\circ},S^{1}\times\Sigma)
—the latter follows from the fact that
A(S^{1})\to A(S^{1}\times\Sigma)
is split injective and rationally
(d-2)
-connected by
[
73
, Prop. 1.1]
. Thus, we see that the rational homotopy groups of the fibre in (
6.7.2
) are finite-dimensional in degrees
1\leq*\leq d-4
, and hence by the long exact sequence and
A
, it follows that
\pi_{d-4}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiff(S^{1}\times\Sigma))
is indeed infinite-dimensional.

Corollary 6.7.6
.

Let
N^{d-1}
be a compact simply-connected spin manifold of dimension
d-1\geq 5
. Then, for each
1\leq*\leq d-4
,
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times N))
is infinitely generated if
\H_{*}(N;\mathbf{Q})\oplus\H_{*+1}(N;\mathbf{Q})\neq 0
. In particular, if
N
is moreover a closed manifold which is either a
2
-connected rational homology sphere or not a rational homology sphere, then there is some
2\leq*\leq d-4
for which
\pi_{*}^{\mathbf{Q}}(\BDiffb(S^{1}\times N))
is infinitely generated.

Proof.

The first claim follows immediately from
F
and the observation that
\widetilde{\H}_{*}(N;\mathbf{Q})\oplus\widetilde{\H}_{*+1}(N;\mathbf{Q})
is a split summand of
\widetilde{\H}_{*}(LN;\mathbf{Q})
for
N
simply-connected by
Lemma

6.7.2
. For the second, the case when
N
is a
2
-connected rational homology sphere is
6.7.5
. Otherwise, there is some degree
2\leq*\leq\lceil\tfrac{d-1}{2}\rceil
for which
\H_{*}(N;\mathbf{Q})\neq 0
. Since for
d\geq 5
we have that
\tfrac{d-1}{2}\leq d-3
, the result follows from
F
.
∎

6.8
Corollaries
G
and
H
: Codimension
2
long knots and topological Stiefel manifolds

In this section, we first prove
G
, and later reinterpret some of our results concerning long knots in terms of the orthogonal functor
\mathsf{Vt}_{2}
of codimension
2
topological Stiefel manifolds.

6.8.1
Proof of
G

Let
d\geq 6
be an integer. We have a decomposition of
D^{2}\subset\mathbf{R}^{2}
as the union of
\frac{1}{2}D^{2}
and
D^{2}\backslash\int(\frac{1}{2}D^{2})
. The latter is diffeomorphic to
S^{1}\times D^{1}.
By taking products with
D^{d-2}
, we have a decomposition of
D^{d}\cong D^{d-2}\times D^{2}
as the union of
\bar{\nu}D^{d-2}\coloneq D^{d-2}\times\frac{1}{2}D^{2}
and
S^{1}\times D^{d-1}.
We will need the following two observations:

1

The parametrised isotopy extension theorem gives a fibre sequence
